### Original Quote History168: Local Repair And Validation

On 14 September 2026 the owner manually authorised only the original Quote
history168 batch. The ChatGPT browser relay remains restricted: no retry,

The fixed inventory remains SHA-256
`e50afdf0d1b2fa7aba6eacc231736a456546116833bf36af79caacc3290fe447`:

and initial focus are seeded; cleanup callbacks are deliberately held. This is
not natural whole-site traversal or physical-device validation.

Two test-baseline defects were reproduced and repaired only in
`tests/e2e/phase3e2-quote-history.spec.ts`:

- Chromium's sticky-header `locator.click()` scrolled before delivering input.
  The original expectation used pre-driver y312 while input, modal lock and
  restoration were y0. A read-only trusted-pointer receipt now captures each
  opening position independently, checks one trusted left-button event, and
  verifies the captured lock against it. No force click or corrective scroll
  was added; restoration observations still use the existing strict helper.
- Windows WebKit's deliberate Call-link focus was still smoothly scrolling
  when the chosen-focus baseline was sampled (y23878 to y23822). The test now
  observes natural settling before capturing that baseline, verifies the stale
  callback is still held and the chosen control remains focused, then releases
  it and requires exact focus/scroll/style preservation. Timeouts are unchanged.

Original assertions, case titles, profiles and route scope remain intact.
Production files, shared helpers, dependencies and build33 export are unchanged.
The revised strict guard permits only this standalone test fingerprint delta;
all other source, owner-checkout, ref, dependency and export checks remain.

### Navigation37 Readiness Correction

The mobile-WebKit failure is now traced to the test's seeded preparation racing
the menu's initial focus lifecycle. In the diagnostic, Close menu stayed focused
while the test scrolled Services into view at panel scrollTop 530. The pending
focus-visibility callback then called `scrollBy({top: -514})`, returning the
panel to 16. The exact caller is the exported navigation-focus-visibility code,
not a guessed native scroll. Ordinary early key, wheel and touch cancellation
remain covered by the existing ownership tests and strengthened race fixtures.

The smallest correction changes two test files only. The navigation test now
asserts the initial Close-menu focus, observes natural frame settlement and
checks its visibility without correcting position before seeded preparation.
The audit test retains the original-body inverse hash and adds ownership and
readiness regressions. No production, shared helper, asset or dependency changes
were required. Action/expect/navigation/aggregate limits remain unchanged.

The uninstrumented temporary single-case regression passed in 175,243.359ms;
173 first-party requests equal local forwards with zero unauthorised forwards.
The two reproduced failures and all their requests remain separately reconciled.
The original failed profile remains 10 passed, one failed and 17 unexecuted,
not a partial passing inventory. Source-bound findings are retained locally in
`ev3e2-navigation36-readiness-findings-01.json`, SHA-256
`92518cf6b39c9eeb0e66c015f81cbdda60921c8c1d4e71bf7ecb146e1f71403e`.

### Current Header Gate

11 September 2026. The owner-requested sanitised checkpoint was delivered once
to the exact authorised supervisor conversation, relay ID
`ev3e2-harness35-owner-relay02`. Its complete marked reply continues only Phase
3E2 on `codex/whole-site-completion`. Fresh origin fetch and the source/owner
guard passed before delivery and after receipt. No main deployment is authorised.

Harness35 promoted the qualified screenshot/scroll lifecycle repair and a finite
2,400,000ms aggregate budget for the identified high-DPR WebKit cases. Individual
action, assertion, navigation and settling limits were not changed. Its 212
focused and 1,386 audit-unit tests, lint and full nonincremental TypeScript passed.
The preceding incremental TypeScript run failed only while writing its cache
(TS5033/EPERM); that failed evidence remains retained. Source checking was not
disabled: `tsc --noEmit --incremental false` performed a full successful check.

The supervisor's observation-boundary review found that `quoteCycle` performed
its final visibility check using a helper permitted to scroll after Back/Escape.
Although focus and scroll-lock restoration were already asserted first, that
last visibility assertion could still reposition a poorly restored control.
Observation36 adds an explicit observation-only helper mode for this check.
It never scrolls, focuses or activates the target; it retains stable-hit checks
and requires the full bounds to be visible. Interaction preparation still uses
the earlier qualified single-scroll path. Natural Tab/Shift+Tab observations use
the existing read-only focus measurements, not interaction preparation.

Nine additional focused cases prove that observation does not repair offscreen,
partially clipped, covered, moving or newly occluded controls, permits genuine
natural settling, and follows the focus/restoration assertions. All 221 focused
tests and all 1,395 audit-unit tests passed, with zero failures, cancellations,
skips or todos. Lint, full nonincremental TypeScript and `git diff --check` passed.

## Historical Build33 / Test-Budget34 Investigation

11 September 2026, continuing the 04:36Z and 05:35Z diagnostic batches. A focused
Windows WebKit observation found eight identical target rectangles before and
after a redundant scroll attempt, with no observed target/ancestor animation or
transform. Frame intervals were 1.599-1.948 seconds at 2560px, DPR3 and simulated
200% root text. The installed Windows WebKit driver requires five stable animation
frame comparisons before scrolling. Its 10-second action limit was exceeded on
an already visible, hit-clear Quote control. This is a measured laboratory
harness condition, not a claim about physical mobile-device performance.

A temporary helper avoided that redundant scroll only when finite, positive
geometry was fully inside the unscaled visual viewport and every sampled hit was
clear. The original post-scroll stable-hit and focus assertions remained intact.
One positive/15 negative predicate fixtures and seven lifecycle scenarios passed.
The focused two-control browser diagnostic passed in 60.647 seconds: required
first-control scrolling was retained, and the already-visible second-control
check took 164ms. Its proof is
`47441222ab0ebf7f47d2c4277316d270046918855c71246559dd7c353c6eeeb4`.
Each focused run reconciled 48 first-party requests with 48 loopback forwards,
one inert outside observation and zero unauthorised forwards. The earlier
observation's intentionally recorded failed action remains explicitly failed.

The complete copied case still failed after 446.730 seconds, this time on a
genuinely off-screen control: y=5533.671875, height=56, viewport height=1080, with
all five hit points outside. The driver's pre-scroll stability wait timed out at
9879.427ms. Its 51 first-party requests equal 51 permitted local forwards, one
outside observation was inert, and the reconciler retains FAILED status rather
than treating network containment as functional success. Skipping redundant
scrolling alone is therefore not a complete repair.

The next temporary full-case diagnostic changes only the necessary scroll call
to a single native DOM `scrollIntoView` with instant/center/nearest options. It
retains visibility, original post-scroll stability/hit assertions, focus checks,
trusted activations, all functional steps and all existing action/assertion/
navigation/settling limits. No repeated repositioning, force click, overlay change
or synthetic activation is used. One positive/15 negative predicate fixtures and
nine lifecycle scenarios pass, including rejection of still-offscreen, covered,
moving, newly occluded and hidden controls and propagation of scroll errors.
The original post-scroll observation block is byte-identical. Preparation:
`d4aa4f225c441eed18496046164f925600b1a2ebcb27c21a255a1adc6403f931`.
The full copied case passed in 1,810,019ms with no failed browser actions, retries
or skips. It completed all original header, main/footer keyboard, Quote, history
and restoration checks. Independent parsing retained the unchanged functional
validator and rejected all 50 corrupted case fixtures. Source/configuration
inverses and the earlier failed case were also verified. This is not promoted
repository code or original-suite acceptance; the actual accepted header count
remains 88, with three complete WebKit profiles still pending.

All 130 scroll decisions were reconciled: 32 required a single native scroll and
98 were already visible. All 130 post-scroll observation groups passed the
original stable geometry and hit checks. Native scroll calls totalled 2,215ms,
with a maximum of 131ms. All 5,107 operation pairs reconcile; 132 unsuccessful
inner polling samples remain recorded within passing outer assertions. Three
timing mutations, four capture mutations, one decision mutation and three journal
mutations were rejected. The unmodified screenshot attachment decodes to the
expected DPR3 dimensions with nonblank pixels; no new physical-device or
every-pixel visual-review claim is made.

Network reconciliation passed: 295 first-party observations equal 295 permitted
loopback forwards, three outside observations were inert, and there were zero
unauthorised forwards or unexplained runtime errors. The durable journal matches
the final inventory exactly. No real form or conversion was delivered.

Primary proof SHA-256:
`8156e01cce88f7ee18f4b73f08c17a5ee617df539c06f0a89bd3ad5487dd96e6`.
Network proof SHA-256:
`a498e64f18c2b77835fe0a886c84f469e4d1f3cd83f5432cd56ed28e3666acec`.
Timing supplement SHA-256:
`be1f74cd969144b285e5245edfba013f820fae1c1c9eef7edaee593145133bb2`.
The first independent-reader attempt failed because host-realm arrays and VM
arrays have different prototypes under strict equality. Parsing the unchanged
report JSON inside the validator's VM fixed that reader-only issue; no assertion
was removed and all 50 negative fixtures still reject.

The measured case exceeds the unchanged original 1,800,000ms aggregate budget by
10,019ms. The temporary 5,400,000ms budget is finite diagnostic evidence, not an
accepted original-test result. Promotion must use a finite evidence-led aggregate
budget with reasonable headroom, retain all individual action/assertion limits,
qualify the source changes and rerun affected gates. Do not count this copied
case as completing the mobile-Safari, iPad or iPad-Pro profiles.


cumulative waits; the recorded case was progressing, not indefinitely hung.
This does not prove an internal WebKit scheduling cause, physical-device latency
or a production website performance defect. No settling assertion is relaxed.

## Build33 WebKit Harness Diagnosis

10 September 2026: the earlier temporary Windows WebKit compositing override
is not suitable for responsive-picture breakpoint acceptance. Omitting the
browser's default `--disable-accelerated-compositing` argument caused stale
`currentSrc` values after narrow 479-to-480px changes. The original production
regression failed all 28 WebKit cases at the source assertion. Its failures are
retained as `a3-b33-brand-clearance-webkit-01`, not relabelled as passes.

The diagnosis used only temporary fixtures and the existing local artwork:

- A five-second source-selection wait still failed all six diagnostic cases.
- Broad 320-to-480px changes passed three cases, but the narrower sequence
  320, 479, 480, 481, 479, 478, 480 failed in all three modes.

  JavaScript held, and after hydration with or without explicit image decode.

  rejected and did not modify the installed Playwright files.

  diagnostic pass all three modes, for both original and cloned pictures.

## Evidence limits

The owner states that EVAREADY supplies electrical, ASP Level 2, solar/battery,
air-conditioning electrical and registered-cabling services. Current documentary
scope, individual competencies and network permissions have not been independently
verified. General regulator guidance is not evidence of EVAREADY authorisation.
No private records, external accounts, customer details or credentials were read.

The following sources support the boundaries in the customer guidance, not a
site-specific design, complete standards review or professional certification.
Manufacturer instructions, the actual connection and current requirements must be
checked for each accepted job. No installation dimensions or DIY procedures are given.

## Route-to-source matrix

All `/services/` names below retain their existing `-sydney` slug endings.

| Route subject | Primary sources | Boundary retained |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Level 2 hub | [NSW ASP scheme](https://www.energy.nsw.gov.au/nsw-plans-and-progress/regulation-and-policy/asp-scheme-and-contestable-works), [Ausgrid authorisation](https://www.ausgrid.com.au/asp-and-contractors/ausgrid-authorisation), [Endeavour authorisations](https://www.endeavourenergy.com.au/working-with-us/authorisations) | Accreditation is not blanket permission for all network activities. |

Fallen-line guidance uses [NSW outage safety](https://www.energy.nsw.gov.au/households/action/power-disruption/outage).
Optional quote photographs never require opening covers, entering roof spaces,
climbing, approaching wires or digging. Private paperwork belongs in an agreed
private channel, not this repository.

The final FAQ reread found an ambiguous emergency/distributor choice in the
overhead-line answer. The [NSW outage guidance](https://www.energy.nsw.gov.au/households/action/power-disruption/outage)
was rechecked on 7 September: immediate danger requires Triple Zero first;
fallen-line clearance and distributor notification remain separate instructions.
The overhead, phone and media-wall photo answers and two process steps now
explicitly make photos optional without concealed-area or hazard inspection.
The underground guide describes the agreed locating/excavation scope without
internal staffing terminology. These are selected-record corrections only.

Natural Chromium Tab traversal also reproduced eight normal-text and thirteen
enlarged-text viewport-edge focus-ring clips on the Level 2 page. Waiting for
scroll settlement did not remove them. A temporary 12px scroll-margin experiment
removed every measured clip without changing layout. The production rule is
limited to controls inside the 21 opted-in `main[data-service-scope]` outputs;
full browser reruns remain required. The failed header run and temporary
diagnostic are retained separately, not counted as final acceptance evidence.

After the service-card repair, natural traversal exposed the same issue in
footer links: one normal-text and seven enlarged-text focus rings clipped the
viewport edge in the 1440px diagnostic. The same 12px scroll margin eliminated
those measured clips. A1's bounded footer-focus correction therefore also uses
that rule, with no text, destination, dimensions, font-size or artwork change.
Failure attachments now retain the actual control and clipping rectangles,
including root-viewport geometry, instead of an empty root class name.

## Currency notes

[NSW service rules](https://www.energy.nsw.gov.au/nsw-plans-and-progress/regulation-and-policy/service-and-installation-rules)
list April 2026 as the current edition; earlier editions are not presented as current.
The full April PDF exceeded the research retrieval limit. Its publication status and
[change summary](https://www.energy.nsw.gov.au/sites/default/files/2026-05/Summary-of-changes-Service-and-Installation-Rules-NSW-April-2026.pdf)
were checked, not every design clause.
[Current electrical documentation guidance](https://www.nsw.gov.au/housing-and-construction/compliance-and-regulation/electricians/electrical-compliance-requirements)
must be used instead of old paper-form instructions. Customer copy deliberately
does not promise a particular authority's acceptance or a universal certificate.
ACMA permits compliant completion statements, not only its TCA1 form.

| `intercom-access-control-electrician-sydney` / `intercom-installation-sydney` | `needs_search_console_evidence` | Access infrastructure versus station installation is a possible distinction, but operational scope/security authorisation and demand must be verified. |
| `tv-antenna-wall-cabling-sydney` / `tv-points-antenna-electrician-sydney` | `consolidate_later` | Existing packages overlap. Media-wall planning versus an outlet change is explained without inventing reception services; no surviving URL is selected without demand/backlink review. |

No routes, redirects, canonicals, robots or sitemap entries are changed. No Search
Console, advertising, revenue or backlink information was accessed or invented.

## Scope-aware rendering

The existing service renderer injects credential bundles, response claims and
generic photo requests beyond the route record. Selected records opt into
`scopeBoundary`; the renderer uses it for visible qualification and safe intake,
and avoids adding unrelated specialist credentials to their local schema.
Optional props on the existing process/lead panels are necessary to prevent the
old claims from being reinserted. Absence must preserve all old defaults and DOM.
No header artwork, footer wording, conversion tracking, destinations or offer terms are changed.

## Bounded amendments

The approved Phase 3E2 A1 amendment covers reproduced footer target-size and
enlarged-text reflow defects. CSS changes preserve its text, links, business
details and normal-motion behaviour. A reduced-motion-only rule removes the
measured CTA/menu/footer transitions. The footer is checked across all 16 route
templates, content-length edge cases, 11 widths and both root-text scales.

The text-fit observer excludes genuinely hidden descendants, including computed
one-pixel, absolutely positioned, clipped screen-reader-only text. A class name
alone is insufficient. Visible clipping, off-screen content and real interactive
surfaces remain checked independently. Fifteen synthetic fixtures also distinguish
the root viewport clipping boundary from a scrolled root-element box when a
fixed dialog locks the body, for both text and focus outlines. Actual off-viewport
dialog text or focus clipping remains a failure.

The approved A2 amendment permits description corrections for exactly
`/services/cctv-security-camera-installation-sydney` and
`/services/intercom-installation-sydney`. Their descriptions also feed two
`/services/` OfferCatalog entries. That route is a mechanically affected,
previously reviewed route, not a 22nd individual review. All other catalogue
entries and the other 979 nonselected register rows must remain unchanged.

Three selected metadata descriptions (CCTV, intercom/access control and intercom
installation) are qualified to remove unsupported installation promises. No
other route metadata, canonical, robots or sitemap change is authorised.

Historical snapshots must retain their old hashes and record reviewed new hashes
for the exact 19 changed records. Unselected records remain guarded. The sealed
107-route semantic comparison is narrowed only by the explicit derived
`/services/` exception, with its two catalogue deltas checked separately.

The pre-edit baseline preserves all 1,001 HTML outputs, 980 nonselected register
rows and per-route header/footer hashes. Its export marker predates the feature
commit; production-source provenance is separate, not inferred from that marker.
Final build, isolation, browser and register validation remain required.

The approved A3 amendment permits bounded menu target-size, wrapping and focus
corrections. It preserves the banner, artwork, ticker, navigation wording, link
destinations, ordering and normal menu/history logic. Actual dropdown links are
44px minimum targets. Mobile group containers no longer clip focus outlines.
The temporary local server uses the exported custom 404 with a genuine 404
status; origin-root probes cannot pass as root-mounted versions of valid pages.

The approved A4 amendment permits sticky-contact suppression while the mobile
menu owns the viewport, a 44px-minimum quote emergency Call control, and the
menu-to-quote cleanup race repair. The prior menu cleanup left inline
`scroll-behavior: auto` until its next frame, which the quote lock could capture
as the original value. It also allowed that stale frame to overwrite a newer
lock. Cleanup now restores the original value synchronously and guards the
queued scroll correction against a new menu or quote lock. No form, destination,
conversion marker, history-entry policy or business wording is changed.

Natural-click tests deliberately hold the real menu cleanup frame while opening
the quote dialog. They cover Escape, Back, the close button, immediate reopening,
empty and nonempty original scroll behaviour, exact style/scroll restoration,
and unchanged active-lock snapshots when the held callback is released. The
fixtures do not generate artificial quote events or bypass hit testing. Screenshots
and full logs stay in local temporary evidence, not in the public repository.

The approved A5 amendment addresses the desktop navigation's fixed 34/40px row,
16px dropdown arrows and absolutely positioned Call/Quote actions. At 200% root
text, the old Home link started at x=-398.12 at 1024px and x=-444.56 at 1440px.
The navigation now wraps in flow, with actual 44px controls. Labels, destinations,
order, artwork, ticker and normal dropdown/history logic remain unchanged.

`DesktopPrimaryNav` observes only its existing `.ev-final-desktop-nav` container.
It writes `--ev-measured-desktop-nav-height` only when the actual border-box height
changes. There is no React state update or observation of the whole document.
The observer disconnects and removes the property on unmount; the property is
also removed below the existing 1024px desktop breakpoint. Navigation sizing does
not consume the measurement, avoiding a resize feedback loop.

The measurement feeds `--ev-desktop-scroll-offset` and desktop `html`
scroll-padding. Main anchor scroll margins are 8px, avoiding double counting.
The earlier A5 implementation also used the measured offset for a fixed-header
spacer, with an 11rem server fallback. The R9 diagnosis above exposed its
hydration movement. The current bounded repair reserves the desktop header in
normal flow, using sticky positioning and a zero desktop spacer; the measurement
and fallback now affect anchor offsets only. Initial-frame, hydration, resize,
native-anchor, scroll and functional validation remain required. These laboratory
observations are not field CLS or Lighthouse.

The initial-frame test temporarily holds only first-party JavaScript requests.
Waiting for document font readiness while deliberately holding document load
deadlocked its first attempt. The repaired fixture measures the styled server
frame without that load dependency, then releases scripts and waits for the real
quote listener. No production assertion was removed. Main and footer are the
actual existing anchors on the tested Level 2 page; no extra IDs are fabricated.
The temporary proxy records its final decisions after closing, including denied
malformed requests and late teardown observations.

The approved A6 amendment covers only Quote cleanup ownership and focus scheduling.
Native Back/fragment completion could clear the opener focus restored inside
`popstate`. The no-fragment reproduction passed, while `#site-footer` left focus
on BODY. The expanded matrix also exposed `#main-content` landing on the existing
non-interactive `main[tabindex="-1"]` and restoring its fragment scroll position.
That exact original-fragment landmark is recognised as a browser landing
placeholder, not as an alternative interactive control.

Original inline styles, including scroll behaviour, are restored synchronously.
The final focus/scroll correction is generation-guarded and cancelled by a new
Quote open, unmount, page exit, changed fragment, deliberate pointer/key input or
focus on another control. It also checks the current URL, connected/focusable
opener and active document locks. It cannot restore a stale lock's styles or
history. Tests retain cancelled callbacks and deliberately invoke them against
new Quote/menu locks and intentional focus choices. All URL fragments, genuine
form destinations, modal names, tracking markers and history policy are retained.

The final 320px/200% testing-report card failure came from an older generic-grid
selector overriding the intended scoped padding: computed side padding remained
24px, leaving 238px for a 241.17px word. The corrected selector targets only
reviewed service scope cards and supplies 16px side padding without changing the
text size. Diagnostic runs retain both computed ancestors and matched CSS rules.

Three selected guide bullets now describe private enquiries and removing unrelated
account details in customer-facing language. Internal "public evidence" and
"repository" wording is excluded from the 21 public service outputs by a focused
regression assertion. Only the three reviewed record snapshots were updated;
their original historical snapshots remain retained.

The final browser observer records console errors, uncaught errors, HTTP failures
and every first-party cancellation. A controlled native-fetch diagnostic showed
that Chromium can report `net::ERR_ABORTED` after successful Next.js HEAD prefetch
headers; explicit HEAD/GET reads returned 200 and the expected byte counts.
Cancellation is not automatically ignored. The classifier requires a unique
matching request and observed native receipt. HEAD has no response body. For a
Next prefetch GET, the observer additionally requires either a complete native
stream read matching the response Content-Length or an observed consumer cancel
on that response stream. Ambiguous matches, unknown failures, missing receipts,
ordinary GET cancellations, HTTP errors and console/runtime errors fail the gate.
All classified cancellations remain in the local result inventory.

The stream observer delegates the original fetch, clone, tee, reader and cancel
calls; it does not consume bodies, suppress errors or change production code.
It observes only first-party responses. Page observations end before controlled
context teardown; separate fail-closed proxy evidence retains teardown decisions.
Classifier tests reject duplicate/ambiguous matches, differing requests, missing
headers, bad statuses, incorrect byte counts and unobserved cancellations.

The A7 amendment distinguishes a proven Windows WebKit capability limitation
from production keyboard defects. In a minimal plain-HTML fixture, Windows
WebKit 26.4 skips ordinary anchors with both Tab and Alt+Tab, but traverses buttons
and inputs. The real navigation behaves identically without negative tabindex,
inertness or hidden anchors. An earlier Mac Option-Tab assumption was incorrect:
[Playwright's pinned focus test](https://github.com/microsoft/playwright/blob/v1.60.0/tests/page/page-focus.spec.ts)
restricts that shortcut test to macOS. Failed runs remain recorded, not recast as
passes. The corrected local diagnostic passed after its initial mistaken route
was replaced with the existing Level 2 route; the initial 404 failure is retained.

A plain-HTML capability canary must precede the focused keyboard assertions.
Full natural forward/reverse traversal remains mandatory for Chromium and Firefox.
For the demonstrated Windows WebKit limitation, only native non-anchor traversal
may be counted as passing sequential traversal. Individual anchor focusability,
unclipped focus indication and trusted Enter activation are checked separately.
Activation delivery is intercepted locally to prevent calls, external navigation
or genuine conversions; destination correctness remains a separate URL contract.
These focus calls are never described as sequential traversal. The impossible
all-anchor assertion is recorded as an unsupported environment capability, not
a pass. No production tabindex, keyboard emulation, operating-system installation,
dependency upgrade or browser-preference change is permitted.

Windows Playwright WebKit profile labels do not mean actual Safari was tested.
Physical macOS Safari full-keyboard-access traversal has not been performed, nor
are the emulated iPhone/iPad profiles physical-device evidence. Final acceptance
still requires the remaining A1-A7 functional, layout, audit and containment gates.

The strong footer rerun exposed a genuine Next.js prefetch error: the Sitemap
entry used `Link`, so entering the viewport requested the nonexistent
`/evaready-electrical/sitemap.xml/__next._tree.txt` and received HTTP 404. The
actual XML file was present. A8 authorises only this exact footer entry to use
a native anchor with the existing `assetPath` helper. Page-route links remain
Next links. Label, class, order, same-tab behaviour and final destination are
preserved; no RSC resource is fabricated and no error is whitelisted. A source
snapshot reverses only the approved footer replacement and verifies the entire
remaining shared file against its starting hash, including all header code.
Both existing build targets require local-only sitemap navigation/MIME checks;
the branded domain must not be accessed or deployed.

The 106-route historical byte snapshot initially failed only because native
React anchors emit `href` before `class`, whereas the prior Next link emitted
`class` before `href`. Each current footer must contain exactly one complete
expected native Sitemap anchor. The snapshot reverses only that exact attribute
order before applying the original sealed hash; all other footer, header and
main bytes remain checked. The independent 1,001-route semantic comparison also
passes without a footer exception. The shared-source isolation test proves that
the production change is confined to the authorised footer branch.

The strengthened keyboard inventory initially counted closed-disclosure links
whose browser layout boxes remained measurable. The repair respects native
closed `details` semantics while retaining its first `summary` control. A minimal
canary verifies both closed and open inventories. The initial two Chromium
failures remain retained; they are not reported as website keyboard failures.

## A9 Quote Iframe Boundary Repair

The strengthened Firefox traversal check exposed a genuine reverse-Tab defect:
the existing boundary handler called synchronous native iframe focus, but focus
remained on Call during keyboard dispatch. The same native focus call succeeded
outside dispatch. A temporary microtask probe completed ten forward/reverse
cycles against the actual contained modal; it is diagnostic evidence, not final
acceptance. One sandboxed browser-launch timeout is retained separately from the
successful contained diagnostic reruns.

A9 authorises only the existing modal boundary/guard transfer. Native focus is
attempted first. If the iframe did not receive it, one microtask retries only
while the same ownership token, open/mounted dialog, connected visible iframe,
URL, history length/Quote marker and source focus remain valid. Keyboard,
pointer, focus-in/out, history, hash, page-hide, close/reopen and unmount events
invalidate the token. No timer, polling, positive tabindex, new form integration,
dimension, wording or destination change is introduced.

Focused tests distinguish unmodified natural interaction from synthetic held
microtask races. Race fixtures locally force the synchronous failure, hold only
the immediately following callback, and invoke it after a superseding action.
A retained-owner positive control must still focus the iframe; stale callbacks
must make no iframe focus call. The external form is always an inert document.
No physical Safari, actual ServiceM8 form or real conversion is tested. Final
acceptance requires fresh builds and every A1-A9 gate; no Phase 3E2 publication
is authorised by this amendment.

The A9 Firefox rerun passed all focus-transfer race cases but initially reported
four focus-outline failures at footer fragments. Screenshots showed intact
controls. The duplicate geometry helpers had incorrectly clipped a viewport-fixed
modal against the scrolled fixed body's box. Their shared checker now follows
fixed-position containing blocks: intervening overflow is bypassed only until
the actual containing block, while transformed/filter/containment ancestors and
the viewport remain enforced. Adversarial fixtures cover scrolled-body overflow,
ordinary overflow, transformed-ancestor clipping and viewport-edge clipping.
All fifteen fixtures passed in Chromium, Firefox and Windows WebKit.

The WebKit fixture initially exposed actual overflow in its synthetic slash word:
that engine did not supply the assumed automatic break opportunity. The positive
fixture now explicitly permits `overflow-wrap:anywhere`, while the unchanged
nowrap/punctuation and genuine overlong-word fixtures must still fail. No actual
overflow was exempted and no production wrapping rule changed for this repair.

The fresh focused Quote boundary suites each passed 30 cases in Chromium,
Firefox and Windows WebKit. They include natural first-open/reopen cycles at
normal/enlarged text and four fragment states, plus positive-owner and stale
microtask fixtures. WebKit's frame/control traversal is recorded separately from
its unsupported all-anchor sequential traversal. These results do not replace
the remaining full-matrix, footer, navigation, mobile-profile or audit gates.

## A10 Focus Visibility

The next full Firefox run found a separate, genuine clipping defect during
natural main/footer traversal. Forty main controls and 51 footer controls were
observed at normal and 200% text. After scrolling settled, Firefox left 7/13 main
controls and 1/6 footer controls partly outside the viewport. The existing 12px
scroll margin fixed Chromium but not Firefox. A root scroll-padding experiment
also left failures. Those failed runs and temporary comparisons are retained.

A10 authorises a separate focus-visibility effect in the existing
`RouteScrollManager`, restricted to focus-visible controls in the selected
service mains and the footer. It does not change focus, tab order, markup, URLs,
document styles or the pre-existing route-scroll effect. A source-isolation
test removes only the new installation/effect and checks the original file hash.

One guarded animation frame revalidates the actual active control, URL/history,
visibility, scope and overlay ownership. It reads existing scroll padding,
scroll margins, the measured fixed header and any visible fixed sticky CTA.
Only a clipped control receives non-animated nearest scrolling, followed by at
most one measured clearance correction. There is no timer, polling or retry
loop. Focus, keyboard, pointer, wheel, touch, history, hash, page-hide, route
cleanup, unmount and menu/Quote ownership invalidate pending work.

The isolated ownership fixtures invoke the same production handler and retain
cancelled callbacks to test that stale invocation cannot scroll. Their initial
Chromium run exposed two fixture mistakes: pointer mode inherited the earlier
keyboard-focused endpoint, and inert-element native blur occurred between
separate snapshots. The fixtures now start pointer mode without the preceding
Tab and capture before/callback/after atomically. The unchanged assertions pass
all 60 cases in each of Chromium, Firefox and Windows WebKit. These are isolated
fixtures, not full-site acceptance, real browser Back or physical-device tests.

Fresh built-site acceptance remains pending. It includes native Tab/Shift+Tab
from the real Skip link without programmatic focus, short and tall viewports,
normal/enlarged text, every selected service route, and all earlier A1-A9 gates.
No Phase 3E2 commit or publication is represented as complete by this document.

## A11 Compact Card Links

The A10 natural-keyboard pilot found controls that cannot physically fit in a
short enlarged-text viewport. At 320x640/200%, a Level 2 whole-card anchor was
715.20px high between a header ending at 140.55px and a visible sticky bar
starting at 421.07px. At 1440x640/200%, the same card was 540.80px high while
the header ended at 348.43px. Both strict visibility assertions failed. No
scrolling algorithm can make those entire focus boxes visible simultaneously.

A read-only 42-case inventory covered all 21 routes at both widths. Its first
multi-navigation test correctly failed request reconciliation because repeated
fetch keys were ambiguous. Separate test contexts removed that ambiguity without
changing the classifier. The next inventory mistakenly included a CSS-hidden
desktop sticky bar; explicit visibility checks corrected that diagnostic. The
final isolated inventory passed all 42 cases. It found oversized main links on
all 21 routes in at least one measured condition and no oversized footer links.

A11 covers the reproduced Level 2 service, when-to-call and related-link groups;
selected shared-service related, switchboard and Level 2 groups; split-system
load-capacity links; and solar related-service cards. Their substantive headings,
descriptions, icons, sequence and destinations remain intact. Each converted
card has one compact, descriptively named native Next link. Existing `View
service` or `Learn more` wording is reused; `View service` is added only where
there was previously no concise action. The shared renderer uses the new
structure only when its existing Phase 3E2 `scopeBoundary` is present.

No stretched-link overlay or click-handler proxy is used. Card body text remains
selectable; the explicit compact action is the interactive target. Existing
native link context-menu and new-tab behaviour are retained. The narrow Level 2
hero and final Call/Quote groups lose only excessive horizontal padding on small screens;
wording, icons, font sizes, destinations and conversion markers remain unchanged.

A sealed, public-source-only fixture records the pre-A11 build12 link order,
content blocks and SEO contracts for all 21 routes. It describes its uncommitted
source honestly rather than claiming a clean-commit run. Tests must prove no
lost or duplicate links, unchanged substantive copy and metadata/schema, one
native action per converted card, and unchanged legacy rendering when disabled.
Full built-site pointer, keyboard, short/tall viewport and earlier phase gates
remain pending; none of these changes has been published.

The card contract initially failed on three paragraphs because the new test
discarded React HTML comments while the sealed baseline parser retained them.
Matching the original parser options passes all 21 exact contracts without
changing the fixture. Clean local root build15 and preview build16 pass, followed
by all 19 static audits, TypeScript and 1,082 audit-unit tests. The all-route
semantic comparison also passes. These are uncommitted-source checks, not final
clean-candidate evidence.

The broader Chromium natural-focus run was deliberately interrupted after
17 passes and one failure, with the remainder incomplete. Its first Call target
at 320x640/200% was 259.03px tall and overlapped the visible sticky bar. The
subsequent build16 extends the authorised narrow padding correction to that
hero group. The interrupted run is not an accepted sweep.

Eight long-text, reduced-motion and native text-selection fixtures pass. The
new navigation pilot retains four failures: after the real Skip link and a
cross-route link, Back restores the source URL but can leave destination content;
ordinary pointer navigation returns the correct content but loses the originating
link focus and scroll position. Tests now wait for the destination heading, then
verify the original heading after Back, instead of treating a URL change as a
completed render. A temporary native-anchor experiment also fails focus and
strict cancellation reconciliation, and is not an accepted workaround. These
history failures require diagnosis and a narrowly authorised same-phase repair;
they are not waived by the passing static checks.

## A12 History Diagnosis

A12 permits only the existing Skip-link integration, narrowly scoped selected
card return handling in RouteScrollManager/a small helper, focused tests and
documentation. No newer release is authorised.

The complete Chromium and Firefox comparisons each record 14 failing sequences with no additional
runtime/request failures: no fragment, the actual Skip link, and an explicit
native footer-fragment entry, with pointer/Enter, normal/enlarged text and two
Back/Forward cycles. Two controls use the unchanged footer Find-your-suburb link
instead of an A11 card. Both reproduce the wrong-DOM result, excluding the new
card structure as its cause. There is no existing source link to `#site-footer`;
that case is explicitly a native fragment-navigation diagnostic, not claimed
existing customer UI. The first diagnostic attempted to query a source link
index in the wrong destination DOM; a nonblocking identity observation fixed
that evidence error while retaining the failing focus assertions.

Without a fragment, Next router state is present and Back restores the source
H1, but focus returns to BODY and scroll to zero. Native fragment entries have
null history state. After cross-route navigation, Back restores their URL while
the destination H1 remains visible; Forward leaves that same destination. The
installed Next 16.3.0 app-router popstate handler explicitly returns without a
restore action when event.state is null. RouteScrollManager's pre-existing
pathname effect also resets scroll on return navigation and has no selected
origin-focus restoration. This is measured router/history integration behaviour,
not a first-party 404, altered RSC file or a timeout to hide with longer waits.

The intended minimal correction is framework-aware Skip-link integration that
retains its literal hash href and accessibility behaviour, plus a single-use,
in-memory bookmark for selected compact-card departures. Any namespaced history
identity must preserve the current Next-owned state. Only the exact matching
Back entry may restore the original control and scroll after the correct source
DOM commits; later input, overlays, navigation or stale callbacks must cancel it.
Normal forward navigation and unrelated controls must keep their prior behaviour.
The first uncommitted candidate used Next Link for the existing Skip link. Its
pilot passed 10 checks but failed both natural keyboard checks: the main target
never received focus. That candidate is rejected. The revised candidate restores
the complete original root layout and native Skip link. A scoped hash handler
preserves the opaque router state only for a null `#main-content` or `#site-footer`
entry on the same committed selected page, matching its pathname, scope and H1.
It does not prevent native navigation, focus or scrolling. A namespaced opaque
history identity associates one in-memory public
route/link/scroll bookmark with its source entry; it is not a new history entry.
For a null native fragment entry, only the opaque Next router state from the same
committed source may be retained. No RSC tree is fabricated or modified. The
controller does not intercept navigation, reload pages, reset history or persist
cross-session data. Queries and unrelated source fragments are not bookmarked.

An exact matching Back entry suppresses only that return's old page-top reset.
Correct source DOM, scope, heading, link position, href/name, history identity,
visibility and overlay ownership are rechecked before a single guarded frame
restores the saved scroll and focus with preventScroll. The record is consumed;
later repeated history traversal does not reuse it. Input, navigation, overlays,
disconnection and disposal cancel pending restoration. A bounded inverse-diff
test retains the original scrolling source hash, and a second test proves the
complete root layout matches its original hash. Full A12 acceptance
and all earlier phase gates remain pending.

The revised native-Skip pilot passed all 12 checks. A 32-case Chromium suite
then verified no-hash, Skip and native-footer entries, normal/enlarged text,
short/tall viewports, pointer/Enter, repeated Back/Forward, unchanged footer-link
controls, direct loads and refreshes. The first selected Back must restore the
exact link and saved viewport; a consumed bookmark is not reused on later cycles.
These are local candidate checks, not committed-source or deployed evidence.

Repeated full loads revealed a limitation in the earlier request observer: it
required each URL/method to occur only once in an entire test. The observer now
assigns each native fetch a nonprivate per-document/call identifier. Repeated
HEAD groups pass only when every network response and every distinct native
receipt is 200, with exactly matching counts. Repeated Next prefetch groups
additionally require every native call to show a complete body of the exact
declared byte size or its own observed consumer cancellation. Partial, duplicate,
unmatched, wrong-status, wrong-size and non-prefetch groups remain failures.
This is a complete identical-request group proof, not an invented individual
pairing. Every request and cancellation remains in evidence; external containment
and website functional assertions are unchanged. Four adversarial unit tests
cover the original and extended evidence rules.

The 48-case synthetic controller ownership suite initially passed 46 and failed
the two same-task Quote-open/close cases. Checking only the final body class
missed the brief ownership transfer. The correction observes the previous class
value as well, so pending work is cancelled even if the overlay has already
closed. The fixture also caught an overloaded TypeScript scroll-observer typing
error; a signature-preserving Proxy fixes that test code. Native website history
tests and synthetic ownership fixtures are reported separately. The synthetic
local document contains no owner information, real integration or RSC file.

## A12 Static-Resource Reconciliation

The build-19 history and isolated ownership runs passed 368 functional cases,
but their combined request reconciliation failed. That combined result is not
accepted evidence. WebKit-family repeated document loads reported additional
image, script and sometimes CSS request events that had no corresponding new
proxy forward. The discrepancy is retained, not dismissed as a successful test.

A read-only three-document diagnostic captured the exact response bodies,
request timings and Resource Timing entries. Initial deliveries had connection
and request timings and transferred bytes. Later observations for those same
resources had no DNS, connection or request-start timing, identical response
start/end, zero-transfer Resource Timing and exactly the same decoded body hash.
This is evidence of local static-resource reuse, not a new upstream delivery.
No service worker was involved. The diagnostic did not disable caching, alter
resource URLs, filter requests or change the site's responses.

The observer now retains body hashes and timing evidence for static resources.
A reuse classification is restricted to WebKit, successful same-origin GETs in
the preview's images/static directories without a query, and a previously
observed successful network response with identical bytes. It also requires a
unique matching zero-transfer Resource Timing entry, no worker, no failure,
completed response and absent connection/request timing. The offline reconciler
recomputes the classification, checks the body against the exact served export,
and separately reconciles all remaining request events with proxy forwards.
Duplicate or incomplete evidence fails closed. Cached observations remain in
the inventory and are not described as network forwards or hidden cancellations.

The fresh desktop WebKit history run passed 32 cases: 2,498 first-party events
comprised 2,480 authorised loopback forwards and 18 proven local resource reuses.
There were zero reconciliation findings, runtime errors or unauthorised forwards.
Mobile and tablet reruns must be verified separately before inclusion. Five
focused evidence unit tests pass, including adversarial missing, ambiguous,
wrong-body, wrong-status, worker, transport and unsupported-engine cases.
Lint and TypeScript pass. This remains uncommitted candidate evidence; earlier
browser runs with the old shared observer cannot replace final clean-SHA checks.

The mobile and 768px tablet history suites also passed their 32 functional cases.
The 1024px tablet run passed 30 but failed both destination refresh cases. Its
trace records four local tree-prefetch errors immediately after navigation away
from the source document, although Playwright network-idle had already resolved.
The native-fetch observer now records call starts/rejections and exposes a
read-only list of unresolved native promises. Settled-refresh checks require
that list to empty before and after network-idle. No rejection is suppressed,
and these are deliberately settled-refresh tests, not interrupted-load coverage.

The new four-case 1024px refresh diagnostic passed three but retained one
unexplained cancelled HEAD request with no successful response. It must not be
reclassified as a completed HEAD. Running that exact destination/200% scenario
alone in a fresh browser process passed. This narrows the investigation toward
browser/test lifecycle state, but one isolated pass is not a proven root-cause
resolution or a substitute for complete coverage. Both failing runs remain
unaccepted. The full phase is still uncommitted and unvalidated; no production
source was changed for this network-evidence investigation.

## A12 Continuous-Quiescence Repair

Timestamped native observations resolved the next timing question. In the failing
1024px diagnostic, the source boundary reported an empty pending list at epoch
1788770447175ms. Four native HEAD calls started 22-23ms later and were rejected
40-41ms after that snapshot as the test left the document. The instantaneous
empty list and an already-resolved network-idle event did not establish that
layout-triggered prefetch scheduling had settled. The earlier isolated-process
pass was not sufficient to establish a cross-context browser defect.

The settled-refresh prerequisite now waits for the existing natural layout/font
settling check and requires at least 500ms of continuous native-fetch inactivity,
with no unresolved local fetch promises. Starts, responses, body completions,
cancellations and rejections reset that observed activity time. It rechecks the
condition after network-idle and records the boundary. Destination-link metadata
is read before the settling boundary. This adds a readiness requirement rather
than ignoring a cancellation, changing an error assertion, extending a generic
timeout or changing production request/navigation behaviour. Interrupted-load
coverage remains a separate requirement, not something these settled cases prove.

The complete four-case 1024px refresh set passed with exact request reconciliation
after this correction. Seven focused unit tests pass, including boundary-time
adversaries and an isolated observer test proving response/rejection identity is
preserved, unresolved calls are tracked, response bodies are not consumed, and
outside-origin calls are not added to the local observation scope. A full32-case
1024px history rerun and the refresh set in the other six browser profiles are
running separately; they must pass before the batch is recorded as complete.

The 56-case run passed functional assertions, but its offline reconciliation
rejected seven missing HTTP response statuses in Firefox. All seven were at the
tail of the post-refresh document inventory; native fetch starts had been
recorded without their later receipts. The shared fixture drained binding
messages but removed listeners while local requests were still in progress.
This is an evidence-collection cutoff, not proof of a successful response.

The fixture now retains listeners until all observed local requests reach a
terminal event, static-body observations finish, and native/request activity
has been continuously quiet for 500ms. It records the before/after collection
boundary and asserts response-inventory completeness inside Playwright as well
as in the offline reconciler. Collection timeouts remain failures with evidence;
no missing response, cancellation or console error is silently accepted.
The original failed reconciliation is preserved and is not accepted batch evidence.

The completed current-observer history batch passes all 224 cases: 32 each in
desktop Chromium, Firefox, Windows WebKit, mobile Chromium, mobile WebKit, iPad
768px and iPad Pro 1024px profiles. There are zero failed or skipped cases and
zero offline reconciliation findings. The 13,847 first-party observations equal
13,787 authorised loopback forwards plus 60 separately proven local static
resource reuses. All 242 outside-origin observations were intercepted by Layer 1;
there were no unauthorised external forwards. The exact base URL remains
`http://127.0.0.1:4214/evaready-electrical/`. Node is 22.23.1, Chromium 148.0.7778.96,
Firefox 150.0.2 and Windows WebKit 26.4. WebKit anchor-focus capability limits under
A7 remain explicit; emulated device profiles are not physical-device coverage.

Exact commands, source/export hashes and per-case evidence remain in the retained
`a12-b19-drained-history-PROFILE-01` runs. The complete reconciliation JSON SHA256
is `3d1b909941f8ff6e5edb615f55e8429a2dbf9017e73b110a0f77e88ebd725e59`.
The earlier four-case sandbox attempt failed Firefox launch before a page opened;
its log, trace and interruption record are preserved. Elevated local-process
permission resolved that environment issue without changing the proxy, browser
assertions or launch timeout. The successful retry and full batch are new runs.

Current lint, TypeScript, all 19 static audits and all 1,089 audit-unit tests pass.
The fresh 1,001-route semantic isolation comparison passes with no unexpected
deltas (JSON SHA256 `25b88de7fd1499801fc45f3b0139a3721eb3e62469b54bafca37fb5e03632303`).
All 873 suburbs retain index/follow, self-canonical and sitemap inclusion; noindex
and redirects remain zero, all 873 decisions remain unreviewed, and approved
evidence remains zero. The owner-decision launch gate remains blocked.

This completes only the native-refresh/network-collection repair batch on the
unchanged build 19 production candidate. It does not complete A12's remaining
natural interruption/ownership integration checks, all earlier phase matrices,
the stale root export replacement, register reconciliation or clean-commit final
evidence. No production edit, commit, push or deployment was made in this batch.

## A9 Route And Component Lifecycle Evidence

The earlier `route` boundary fixture changes a same-document history/hash entry.
It is not an actual Next route transition, and removing the dialog subtree does
not unmount the globally mounted `QuoteFormModal`. These scopes are now tested
separately without changing the production modal or its current build 19 export.

The actual-route tests hold the original guarded iframe-focus microtask, close
Quote normally, and activate a real service card by pointer or keyboard. They
require the exact destination URL and service-record H1, a changed pathname, the
same document identity, the preserved pending callback and correct history state.
Invoking the stale callback must not issue another iframe-focus call, steal
focus, change scroll/locks or change the URL. Chromium and Firefox use natural
Tab from the production-restored opener and trusted Enter. Windows WebKit first
runs the A7 capability canary and explicitly qualifies individual programmatic
anchor focus plus trusted Enter; native all-anchor traversal is not claimed.

All 28 cases pass: pointer/keyboard at 100%/200% in each of seven profiles.
There are no failures, skips or offline reconciliation findings. The 1,254
first-party request observations match 1,254 authorised loopback forwards;
all 56 outside-origin observations were contained at Layer 1. Exact command
and source/export evidence is retained under
`a9-b19-actual-route-PROFILE-02`. The reconciliation JSON SHA256 is
`64cfe467f7a7d4751bc26f7d322be225ce9bd815c9478167cf52e79334830480`.
The complete boundary spec now has 34 cases per profile, including these four;

and the bundle are hashed and independently rechecked by the reconciler.
The two public preview constants are supplied explicitly, not read from secrets.
The test requires `EV3E2_QUOTE_LIFECYCLE_BUNDLE` to identify this verified temporary
bundle; the temporary runner prepares it and records the exact invocation.

The fixture's real `root.unmount()` executes component cleanup. Its eight cases
cover retained ownership, unmount, unmount followed by independent focus, and
unmount followed by a newly mounted/opened modal, each at 100%/200% text. They
verify disconnected old iframe nodes, restored body/document scroll locks,

and unchanged history/URL when that callback is invoked. The positive control
requires a still-valid retained owner to complete exactly one iframe transfer.
This is isolated component-lifecycle coverage with test-only layout and a
programmatically seeded focus boundary, not natural whole-page keyboard or
production visual coverage. The booking document is always an inert Layer 1
fixture with no submission capability.

All 56 lifecycle cases pass across the seven profiles, with zero failures,
skips or reconciliation findings. The 56 synthetic first-party document
responses are explicitly classified as exact Layer 1 fulfilments, not network
forwards; the 70 outside-origin frame observations were also contained at
Layer 1. There were zero unauthorised forwards or Layer 2 rejects in either

`a9-b19-component-lifecycle-PROFILE-04`; reconciliation JSON SHA256 is
`c1268ee12962aad51f30e8bd14e40a1c1b8ad46e4e362a1563d1f1362b46073d`.
Initial harness failures remain retained: the explicit temporary test allowlist
omitted the new file, then the standalone bundle omitted Next's two public

complete runs above are accepted.

After this batch, lint, TypeScript, all 19 static audits and all 1,089 audit

This is uncommitted source-hash evidence, not final clean-commit acceptance.

## A12 Actual Navigation Interruption Evidence

The earlier 48-case controller ownership suite uses synthetic DOM/history
fixtures. New tests now exercise the real rendered Next export: a Level 2
service-card departure, browser Back, and interruption of its pending source
restoration by trusted input, Quote/menu ownership, browser Forward or another
real service-card route. The source/destination URLs, service-record H1s and
unchanged document identity are asserted. The second route must also retain
its own working Back/focus restoration after the obsolete callback is invoked.

The installed TypeScript parser identifies exactly one restoration RAF in the
compiled export. Its chunk and callback are hashed; only that exact callback
is held, and only when the real source URL, H1 and scope have returned. Other
animation frames remain native. A retained-owner positive control must perform
exactly one scroll and one card-focus call. After an interruption, invoking
the obsolete callback must cause no focus, scroll, lock, URL, H1 or history
change. Trusted input events, scroll-call times/stacks and callback cancellation
observations are retained. This is deterministic real-route integration with
one instrumented timing boundary, not uninstrumented timing or physical-device
coverage. The export chunk SHA256 is
`c0c1ba2c326f492e7b9862297aa6c61a8191bc826de9e0275692a0a9766b89fe`.

Three harness findings were diagnosed without changing production code:

- The initial enlarged-text pointer case applied an entire-control, five-point
  hit test to a 900px-tall non-interactive H1. An entire heading need not fit one
  viewport. The case now uses an ordinary non-forced locator click, which still
  requires an actual unobscured hit point. Existing control hit tests are intact.
- The second-route cases initially counted that new route's existing 120ms
  top-reset timer as if it belonged to the cancelled restoration. Retained stacks
  and timestamps identify the new-route reset, including a measured 120.4ms
  interval from the immediate reset in the diagnostic. Comparison now requires
  200ms of continuous scroll-call inactivity first; the exact no-extra-call,
  focus, viewport and history assertions remain unchanged. No timer is suppressed.
- Playwright rejects mouse-wheel input in mobile WebKit. That failed attempt
  remains recorded as an unsupported environment capability, not a production
  failure or a passing wheel test. The applicable mobile/tablet case now uses
  trusted native `touchstart` from a locator tap; desktop cases retain native
  wheel input. Context `isMobile`/`hasTouch` and the actual trusted event type are
  checked. Touch tapping does not establish swipe/drag-scroll or physical-device
  gesture behaviour. The test runner's explicit `EV3E2_PROFILE` must match its
  actual Playwright project, so the applicable input case cannot silently drift.

The current complete matrix passes 140 cases: 20 each in desktop Chromium,
Firefox, Windows WebKit, mobile Chromium, mobile WebKit, 768px iPad and 1024px
iPad Pro profiles. Each profile covers retained ownership, keyboard, pointer,
its applicable wheel/touch input, Quote open/open-close, menu open/open-close,
Forward and a second real route, all at 100% and 200% text. Menu cases explicitly
use a 390x844 viewport. There are zero failed or skipped executed cases and zero

is not included or claimed as passed. Native touch-start coverage and desktop

## Complete Selected-Route Card Matrix

The complete card-link suite passes 644 cases, with zero failures, skips or

Chromium, mobile WebKit, 768px iPad and 1024px iPad Pro profiles. Every profile
covers all 21 selected routes with pointer and keyboard activation at 100% and
simulated 200% root text (84 cases), plus eight temporary long-text cases at
320, 390, 768 and 1440px. These are emulated profiles, not physical-device tests.

The route cases check every card's native action and 44px target, a real internal
destination with a different H1, exact browser Back URL/H1, focus restoration to
the originating action and absence of stale menu/Quote scroll locks. Chromium
and Firefox keyboard cases use the native Skip link and sequential Tab traversal
to the first card action. Windows WebKit cases explicitly use individual
programmatic focus followed by trusted Enter because native sequential anchor
focus is unavailable in this environment. That unavailable capability is not
claimed as passed. Pointer cases use ordinary, non-forced clicks. This matrix
activates the first or last card per route; it is not exhaustive activation of
every card destination or whole-site keyboard coverage.

The long-text cases change only the temporary browser DOM. They retain layout,
minimum-target and reduced-motion assertions, and verify that the original card
paragraph can be selected without activating a link. No synthetic wording is
written into production source or exported files. Test execution times are not
Lighthouse results or field performance measurements.

## Circular Hit Testing And Synthetic Fixture Repair

The first full handoff run passed Chromium's 20 cases, but Firefox passed 14
and failed six before opening the menu. The shared hit-test helper sampled
15%/85% rectangular diagonals, within a fraction of a CSS pixel of a circular
button's boundary. At the observed fractional positions, Firefox returned the
underlying element at one such point. Repeated stable geometry and the retained
failure capture did not show a covering overlay. No production change was made
for this finding.

The helper now recognises a square control whose four computed border radii
cover half its dimensions. Only that circular case uses the centre and eight
radial samples two CSS pixels inside its boundary. Rectangular controls retain
the exact original five samples. All new meaningful samples must be clear;
natural clicks, settled geometry and all functional assertions remain required.
Original samples remain in `legacyHits` as separate diagnostic observations.
The fresh Firefox handoff run retains precisely the six original legacy edge
misses at 320, 430 and 768px with normal text and both initial scroll behaviours,
while all nine circular samples are clear. Its 80 circular observations and
280 rectangular observations satisfy the new and unchanged contracts respectively.

Fourteen synthetic regressions were added to the previous 15 layout fixtures:
three observed fractional circular geometries with real click activation, one
unchanged rectangular case, nine deliberate obstructions (one per sample),
and a fully covered circular control which `unobscured` must reject. All 29
fixtures pass separately in Firefox 150.0.2, Chromium 148.0.7778.96 and Windows
WebKit 26.4: 87 passes, zero failures, skips or retries in the accepted runs.
These are synthetic fixtures, not production route or physical-device coverage.

The original blank-document/setContent setup did not retain the actual native
request-activity observers required by the strengthened collection boundary.
An explicit about:blank navigation also failed that requirement. These attempts
are preserved as failed, not excused as empty network activity. The fixture now
navigates to one exactly fulfilled loopback document at
`http://127.0.0.1:4214/evaready-electrical/__phase3e2-layout-fixture__/`.
Its actual response bytes, SHA-256, CSP and installed observer functions are
recorded and independently reconciled. No fixture is placed in public output.

Firefox then exposed an automatic origin-root favicon request blocked by the
fixture's CSP. That failed run is also retained. An explicit embedded PNG favicon
and `img-src data:` resolve that fixture-only request, while `default-src 'none'`
continues blocking network resources. No console errors are ignored and the
network collector is unchanged. The strict reconciler requires the exact suite,
URL, single successful GET document, matching body hashes/size, exact CSP and
real observer readiness before classifying it as local fixture fulfilment.

--id=ID --run-after-primary-approval`, then the strict offline reconciler.

zero proxy forwards, outside-origin requests, Layer 2 rejects or unexplained
runtime errors. Exact commands, source/export hashes and original failures

## Natural Keyboard Partition And Entry Timing

The natural traversal suite remains 21 routes, 11 widths, two heights and two
root-text scales: 924 cases each in Chromium and Firefox. The temporary runner
now supports one exact sealed route per partition, with 44 required titles. It
rejects invalid indices, duplicate arguments, unrelated suites, other engines
and root-mounted builds. Its self-tests pass four positive checks and reject
18 invalid selections. The complete gate still requires all 42 profile/route
partitions with identical source, export and harness identities; a partial
partition must never be described as full natural keyboard coverage.

The first Level 2 route run, `a7-b19-natural-route00-chromium-01`, passed 43
cases and failed one at 320x640 with 200% root text. Its first Call focus ring
extended to 455.71875px against a sticky-control top of 421.09375px. The original
failure, screenshot, trace and request evidence are retained outside Git.

Observation-only timing diagnostics on the unchanged build identified an entry
race: `mouse.wheel` returned before the browser finished scrolling, and the
native Skip-link `hashchange` could arrive after the first control received
focus. Three of four second-diagnostic attempts recorded that event order.
The existing focus handler deliberately invalidates pending work on navigation.
Diagnostics recorded the existing handler moving the control to a clear
152.6875-411.71875px rectangle; they are observations, not acceptance results.

Only the natural test's entry synchronisation changed: observe a genuine deep
wheel scroll, await settled geometry, observe native Skip-link hashchange and
assert the exact preview URL and main focus before the next Tab. No focus or
scrolling is injected. Every original forward/reverse traversal, active focus,
outline, clipping and fixed-control clearance assertion remains intact. A new
entry attachment retains actual deep-scroll distance and the settled URL.
The initial test-only TypeScript cast error was corrected and the rerun passes.
Full lint, TypeScript and the 10 focused focus/network audit-unit tests pass.

The temporary natural-sequence verifier passes one positive control and rejects
25 malformed fixtures. It requires complete forward/reverse control order,
visible clear focus, observed native entry, exact case identities and separate
request reconciliation. Its partial-result status cannot satisfy the full gate.
The Chromium 02 rerun passed 44 cases and request reconciliation, but Firefox 02
rejected all 44 new entry preconditions: a single native wheel event moved only
part of its viewport (for example, 452px at 360x640). That failed setup is retained,
not presented as 44 website failures or as successful traversal. The final setup
uses at most six genuine wheel events, observes progress and settlement after
each, and retains the same deep-scroll threshold. No `scrollTo` or focus injection
was introduced. Both engines require new identical-source 03 runs; the earlier
Chromium result is not combined with the changed test. The entry evidence now
also records the continuous native-wheel steps and actual resulting scroll.
The subsequent Firefox 03 run failed one native traversal case; Chromium 03
was not started. The production diagnosis and replacement build are recorded
below. Neither earlier Chromium 02 nor Firefox 03 completes the current gate.
No production, header, artwork, build export, dependency or deployment changed
as part of this test-harness repair.

## Firefox Competing Focus Scroll Repair

Firefox's route-00 run 03 passed 43 cases and failed reverse traversal at
820x640 with normal root text. The Smart Meter service-card link's focus ring
sat behind the fixed header. Waiting for stable scrolling did not repair it:
the four-case stable-measurement pilot passed three and failed the same case.
Both failed attempts remain recorded outside Git, with no skipped assertions.

An observation-only pilot captured the exact sequence for that native Shift+Tab:
focusin at scrollY 4266 with target top 12px; frame one at scrollY 4124 with
target top 154px; frame two back at scrollY 4266 with target top 12px. The link
remained active and focus-visible, with no disqualifying ancestor styles. The
handler's `scrollIntoView` and following measured `scrollBy` competed in Firefox;
the delayed nearest scroll undid the fixed-header clearance adjustment.

The correction removes the redundant `scrollIntoView` operation from the scoped
service-focus handler. Native focus retains ancestor-container scrolling; the
handler makes only its one measured viewport correction. The single guarded
animation frame, visibility checks, scope, URL/history checks, overlay ownership
and all input/navigation/unmount cancellation rules remain. No timer, repeated
correction, focus injection, history mutation or header implementation is added.

The source regression now prohibits reintroducing a competing `scrollIntoView`
call. The isolated ownership fixture observes the actual `window.scrollBy`
operation instead, preserving the same exactly-one valid correction and zero
cancelled correction assertions, plus original focus, URL, styles and geometry
checks. The natural traversal test retains every original clearance assertion.
Its diagnostic event logging is enabled only for explicitly identified pilots;
the full-matrix runner disables it and the independent verifier requires that.

Build 21's clean install, dependency audits and lint passed, but TypeScript
correctly rejected a test observer that omitted the optional no-argument scroll
overload. The observer type was corrected; failed build 21 is retained. Fresh
preview build 22 passes clean install, both zero-vulnerability audits, full lint,
production build and TypeScript under Node 22.23.1 / Next 16.3.0. Comparing its
76 production-source hashes with build 19 identifies exactly one changed file:
`components/route-scroll-manager.tsx`. The other 75, including header sources,
content and artwork references, match. The old export remains preserved.

`a7-b22-natural-handler-firefox-pilot-01` passes all four native regression cases,
including the reproduced failure. This is a pilot, not all-route acceptance.
The root-path export still represents build 20 and is not presented as build-22
evidence. All wider Phase 3E2 gates, register reconciliation and clean-commit
validation remain open. No new commit, push or deployment has been performed
in this repair batch.

## Build 22 Ownership And Evidence Collection Results

The first ownership run rejected all 60 Firefox cases at strict network
teardown: its `setContent` document did not initialise the native-fetch observer.
A second attempt explicitly navigated to `about:blank`, but Firefox still lacked
the observer and failed the added observer-presence assertion. Both failed runs
are retained outside Git; their Chromium/WebKit queues were not started. These
are fixture setup failures, not accepted production or containment results.

The final fixture follows the existing local layout-test pattern: one exact
loopback HTTP document response, fulfilled by Layer 1 with restrictive CSP and
no executable page scripts. It records source URL, delivery count, response body
size, received SHA-256, CSP and all three real observer-readiness checks. Nothing
is added to the production export. The shared quietness/teardown barrier, Layer 2
proxy and every ownership, cancellation, focus and geometry assertion remain.

Fresh `a10-b22-focus-ownership-{chromium,firefox,webkit}-03` runs each pass 60
cases: all 30 scenarios at normal and simulated 200% root text. No failures,
skips or retries. Independent reconciliation verifies all 180 exact cells,
matching source/harness/export hashes and the single exact synthetic response
per test. All 180 local responses were fulfilled by Layer 1, with zero proxy
forwards, zero observed external requests and zero unexplained runtime errors.
The verifier's two positive and 20 negative self-tests pass. Its status is
`PASS-180-SYNTHETIC-FIXTURES`, not natural-navigation or whole-phase acceptance.

- Ownership proof: `ev3e2-build22-focus-ownership-proof-03.json`, SHA-256
  `d09c7b767425a704673131d25c8fbe57a9430ac0a4d35b9d66b0c321f5ea159e`.
- Natural Firefox pilot: four literal cells, each 91 controls and 182 complete
  forward/reverse focus observations. All rings clear the fixed controls.
  Strict network proof `ev3e2-build22-natural-pilot-network-02.json`, SHA-256
  `5d80542b7748699a15ed8d79092d360417ae3963d5bd9176816f81accaa47202`.
  Its 600 local requests reconcile; four external observations were inert
  Layer 1 fixtures, not third-party deliveries. The later ownership-test-only
  file delta is explicitly recorded; the executed natural spec, helpers,
  production and export still match. It remains a diagnostic-enabled pilot.
- Validation 20: TypeScript, 19 static audits and 1,089 audit-unit tests pass,

  Full lint and non-incremental TypeScript also pass after the fixture change.
- Semantic comparison covers all 1,001 routes and passes the authorised delta
